### [1] [Interpreting Parton Distributions with Shapley Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02647)
*Raphaël Bonnet-Guerrini,Stefano Carrazza,Stefano Forte,Eva Groenendijk,Vincenzo Piuri,Ramon Winterhalder*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper employs Shapley values to link parton distributions (PDFs) with high-energy observable predictions, enhancing the understanding of data impacts on PDFs and their uncertainties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess how individual PDFs affect theory predictions for collider observables and quantify data impacts on PDFs and associated uncertainties.

Method: The authors model the regression of PDFs from data as a cooperative game, using Shapley values to evaluate the likelihood of agreement between data and predictions based on each PDF.

Result: The method quantifies the influence of data on PDFs in global fits and reveals a reduced sensitivity of the gluon PDF at intermediate values, affecting BSM searches and gluon fusion Higgs cross sections.

Conclusion: The approach not only validates known PDF-data relationships but also suggests enhancements for hyperparameter optimization in PDF determination, particularly for the NNPDF collaboration.

Abstract: We show that Shapley values can be used to trace how individual parton distributions (PDFs) shape the theory predictions for high-energy observables computed from them. This provides a tool for assessing the impact of data on PDFs when determining them, and the impact of PDF uncertainties when using the PDFs to compute collider observables.The Shapley value is computed by treating the regression of PDFs from data as a cooperative game. The PDFs are the players, and the reward is the likelihood ($χ^2$) that characterizes the agreement between data and the predictions obtained from a given PDF, with theory and methodology held fixed. The method is agnostic to the way PDFs have been determined in the first place: for PDFs determined with a black-box AI model it may be used in order to explain the behavior of the model, and for PDFs determined using a fixed parametrization it may be used in order to expose the features and potential limitations of the parametrization. We find that the method recovers known expectations about which data constrain which PDFs in a global fit, while placing them on a more quantitative footing. We demonstrate its effectiveness in two ways. We uncover an unexpected loss of sensitivity of the gluon PDF at intermediate $x$, with potential implications for BSM searches and the gluon fusion Higgs cross section. We also show that the method can be used to improve the hyperparameter optimization procedure currently used by the NNPDF collaboration.

</details>


### [2] [EFT Validity and Truncation Uncertainty from few Nuisance Parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02649)
*Benoît Assi,Adam Martin,William Shepherd*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the next-order uncertainty in Effective Field Theory (EFT) signal rates using a calculable error term at dim-6² in Simplified Minimal Effective Field Theory (SMEFT), streamlining uncertainty modeling with fewer nuisance parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The goal is to quantify the uncertainties arising from truncating the perturbation series in Effective Field Theory, which can impact the reliability of predictions and limits derived from theoretical models.

Method: The authors compute the next-order error term at dim-6² in SMEFT without additional simulation tools, employing a systematic approach to model uncertainty with minimal nuisance parameters derived from Wilson-coefficient scans.

Result: The proposed method significantly reduces the number of nuisance parameters required to account for uncertainties in EFT signal rates, showcasing reductions by an order of magnitude compared to traditional estimates.

Conclusion: By incorporating the calculable dim-6² portion into the signal estimation, the algorithm ensures that bounds are effectively valid and that truncation uncertainties are manageable, enhancing the robustness of high-energy collider analyses.

Abstract: An observable in an Effective Field Theory (EFT) is an expansion in a set of small parameters, no different than any other perturbation series. Truncating such a series expansion leaves the leading dropped term as the dominant source of error, and that term itself contains a calculable portion. We explore the partial calculation of this next-order error, available at dim-6$^2$ in SMEFT with no additional tools needed to simulate it, and explore how to efficiently use that partial calculation to model the next-order uncertainty in full using a minimal number of nuisance parameters. This estimate of the uncertainty of the EFT signal rate naturally imposes EFT validity by ensuring that bounds are driven by kinematic regions where truncation uncertainties are parametrically smaller than the signal. We incorporate the calculable dim-6$^2$ piece into the signal and cover the remaining higher-dimension uncertainty with a small set of nuisance parameters derived from a scan over Wilson-coefficient values. Our algorithm to determine the relevant nuisance parameters and distributions is automation-friendly and applies to arbitrary truncation choices. We then provide multiple examples of its implementation in high-energy collider processes focused on SMEFT truncated at $\mathcal{O}(Λ^{-2})$, where the dim-6$^2$ piece is used to estimate the full $\mathcal O(Λ^{-4})$ dependence. In the examples considered here the reduction of nuisance parameters is appreciable, generically reducing the nuisance parameter count by an order of magnitude compared to naïve estimates.

</details>


### [3] [Muon lifetime and Fermi constant: an update](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02657)
*Alexander Eberhart,Matteo Fael,Alexander Keshavarzi,Daisuke Nomura,Kay Schönwald,Matthias Steinhauser,Thomas Teubner,Aidan Wright*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper refines the muon lifetime prediction, incorporating QED corrections and improved hadronic contributions which significantly reduce uncertainty.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a more accurate prediction for the muon lifetime by analyzing uncertainties and incorporating advanced calculation methods.

Method: Utilizing QED corrections up to order $α^3$ and state-of-the-art hadronic contributions via dispersive methods, the authors calculate a correction factor $Δq$ and derive the reciprocal muon lifetime expression.

Result: The derived correction factor is $Δq=(-4\, 384\, 678 \pm 34)\times 10^{-9}$, resulting in a reduced uncertainty for $Δq$ by an order of magnitude compared to earlier predictions.

Conclusion: The updated Fermi coupling constant is $G_F=1.166\,378\, 59 \, (59) \times 10^{-5} \, \mathrm{GeV}^{-2}$, with future improvements anticipated from enhanced measurements of the muon lifetime and mass.

Abstract: We present an updated prediction for the lifetime of the muon including a detailed analysis of all relevant uncertainties. Our prediction includes QED corrections up to order $α^3$ and state-of-the-art hadronic contributions based on dispersive methods. Radiative corrections and finite-electron-mass effects are parametrized by the correction factor $Δq$ in $τ_μ^{-1}=G_F^2m_μ^5(1+Δq)/(192π^3)$, for which we obtain $Δq=(-4\, 384\, 678 \pm 34)\times 10^{-9}$. This reduces the uncertainty associated with $Δq$ by an order of magnitude compared to the previous prediction at order $α^2$. We use our results to provide an updated value of the Fermi coupling constant, $G_F=1.166\,378\, 59 \, (59) \times 10^{-5} \, \mathrm{GeV}^{-2}$. Further improvements will require better measurements of the muon lifetime and mass.

</details>


### [4] [Dark Matter in the General 2HDM with Extra Top Yukawa Couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02659)
*Leon M. G. de la Vega,Mohamed Krab*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The G2HDM+S model introduces a real scalar singlet as a dark matter candidate, analyzing its implications on Higgs physics and dark matter interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the two Higgs doublet model by adding a scalar singlet for viable dark matter scenarios, specifically top-window dark matter linked to the top quark.

Method: Examine constraints from Higgs signal measurements and cosmological observations; identify parameter space features and propose benchmark scenarios for experimental investigation.

Result: Identified a rich LHC phenomenology influenced by singlet portals and heavy scalar hierarchy, with six benchmark scenarios proposed for various interaction regimes.

Conclusion: The G2HDM+S model allows for distinctive dark matter production channels, particularly through flavor-violating processes involving top-charm couplings.

Abstract: We extend the general two Higgs doublet model (G2HDM), which introduces extra Yukawa couplings, by an additional real scalar singlet (G2HDM+S), providing a viable scalar dark matter (DM) candidate. This setup provide a viable UV completion of top-window dark matter scenarios, in which DM communicates with the standard model predominantly through the top quark. We analyze the constraints on the visible scalar sector from Higgs signal strength measurements and direct searches for additional Higgs bosons at the LHC, and those on the dark sector from cosmological observables, including the observed DM relic density and spin-independent direct-detection as well as indirect detection. Within the surviving parameter space, we identify a rich and diverse LHC phenomenology governed by the interplay between the singlet portal, the extended Yukawa sector, and the heavy scalar mass hierarchy. To facilitate experimental investigation, we propose six benchmark scenarios spanning the singlet-portal, bosonic-cascade, fermiophilic, and flavor-violating regimes. In the latter, the nondiagonal top-charm coupling $ρ_{tc}$ induces the production channel $cg \to tH$, allowing the top quark to serve as a trigger for an otherwise invisible $H\to SS$ signal. This flavor-violating DM production is a distinct feature of the G2HDM+S.

</details>


### [5] [Studying baryon number transport dynamics via hyperon-kaon correlations in $p + \mathrm{Au}$ collisions at $\sqrt{s_{_{\rm NN}}}=20$, $39$ and $62$ GeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02867)
*Siyuan Ping,Xiaozhou Yu,Long Ma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study investigates hyperon-kaon correlations in heavy-ion collisions to understand baryon number transport mechanisms and strange quark pair production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The observation of baryon number transport to hyperons in heavy-ion collisions raises questions about how this occurs and the role of strange quark pair production.

Method: The study employs multiphase transport (AMPT) and Ultra-relativistic Quantum Molecular Dynamics (UrQMD) models to analyze hyperon-kaon correlations in p + Au collisions at various energies, using the Wasserstein distance method for quantification.

Result: Correlations show dependence on beam energy, hyperon emission direction, and detector acceptance, providing a baseline for future measurements without baryon junction mechanisms.

Conclusion: The findings enhance understanding of baryon number transport and will aid in future experiments investigating gluon-junction interactions.

Abstract: The observation of positive net hyperon baryon numbers at mid-rapidity in heavy-ion collisions indicates that baryon numbers from incident nucleons can be transported across a large rapidity gap to hyperons where strange quarks of $s-\bar{s}$ are pair-produced. Consequently, hyperons and kaons are expected to be correlated, providing a sensitive probe of both baryon number transport mechanism and strange quark pair correlation. Such correlation may be used to test the gluon-junction interaction mechanism where a $Y$-shaped gluonic field may carry the baryon number and be responsible for the baryon number transport to hyperons over a large rapidity gap. We present hyperon-kaon correlations as a function of their relative rapidity in $p + \mathrm{Au}$ collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}} = 20$, $39$, and $62$ GeV using a multiphase transport ($\texttt{AMPT}$) model and Ultra-relativistic Quantum Molecular Dynamics ($\texttt{UrQMD}$) models where hyperon-kaon pairs are originated from fragmentation scheme. We quantify the correlation function using the Wasserstein distance method and systematically investigate the correlation dependence on the beam energy, hyperon emission direction and detector acceptance. Our simulation results provide a baseline without the baryon junction mechanism for future experimental measurements.

</details>


### [6] [Revisiting $\bar B^0 \rightarrow Λ_c^+ \bar p$ decay with higher twist corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02876)
*Zhou Rui,Zhi-Tian Zou,Ying Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper analyzes the decays of single-charmed baryons using PQCD, highlighting significant contributions from higher-twist effects and interference between decay amplitudes, which align predictions with experimental data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the mechanisms and contributions affecting single-charmed baryonic decays of $B$ mesons, emphasizing the roles of $W$-emission and $W$-exchange processes.

Method: Utilizing perturbative QCD (PQCD) to calculate decay amplitudes, incorporating higher-power corrections from the hadronic light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs).

Result: Identified significant higher-twist contributions and a destructive interference, notably reducing the predicted branching fraction of $ar B^0 	o Λ_c^+ ar p$ and predicting the branching fraction for $ar B^0 	o arΛ_c^- p$ to be on the order of $10^{-8}$.

Conclusion: The results improve alignment with experimental data and provide new predictions for decay asymmetry parameters, offering insights for future experimental tests.

Abstract: We investigate the single-charmed baryonic decays $\bar B^0 \to Λ_c^+ \bar p$ and $\bar B^0 \to \barΛ_c^- p$, which receive contributions from both $W$-emission and $W$-exchange topologies, within the framework of perturbative QCD (PQCD). Higher-power corrections associated with the hadronic light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) of both the initial- and final-state hadrons are systematically taken into account. We find that these higher-twist contributions play an important role in baryonic $B$ decays and cannot be neglected. A sizable destructive interference between the $W$-emission and $W$-exchange amplitudes is observed, which significantly reduces the predicted branching fraction of $\bar B^0 \to Λ_c^+ \bar p$ and leads to improved agreement with experimental measurements. The doubly Cabibbo-suppressed decay $\bar B^0 \to \barΛ_c^- p$ is studied for the first time. Its branching fraction is predicted to be of order $10^{-8}$, placing it within the reach of future high-luminosity experiments. We further present the first theoretical predictions for the decay asymmetry parameters of both channels, which provide additional observables for testing the underlying decay dynamics and can be confronted with future experimental data.

</details>


### [7] [Majoron Dark Energy via Freezing Induced by Quantum Coherence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03070)
*Keunsu Cheon,Sin Kyu Kang,Jungjai Lee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A new mechanism for Majoron dark energy is proposed involving a non-equilibrium state driven by quantum coherence in sterile fermions, enabling a metastable phase with specific dynamical properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain dark energy behaviors in cosmology using a nonequilibrium mechanism involving Majorons and pseudo-Dirac fermions.

Method: The paper develops a model where a Majoron is coupled to a reservoir of pseudo-Dirac fermions, resulting in a lagged response characterized by a short-memory regime and a specific scalar equation governing the dynamics.

Result: The model demonstrates that the Majoron velocity can be suppressed dynamically, maintaining a response-dominated state even with high intrinsic mass, leading to a metastable phase with equation of state parameter close to -1.

Conclusion: This approach suggests a novel framework for understanding dark energy that contrasts with traditional static models, emphasizing a dynamic and non-equilibrium nature.

Abstract: We propose a nonequilibrium mechanism for Majoron dark energy in which the late-time freezing of a physical Majoron is induced by quantum coherence in a hidden pseudo-Dirac sterile fermion reservoir. The evolving Majoron background derivatively couples to the hidden pseudo-Dirac number current and drives a lagged reservoir response with a finite memory time. In the short-memory regime, the causal response kernel reduces to \(\dot X+Γ_{\rm PD}X=β\ddotφ\). The leading linear-response matching \(Q=αX\) then yields an effective scalar equation containing the exchange structure \(q_{\rm exch}\ddotφ/\dotφ\). We show that this term can dynamically suppress the Majoron velocity and sustain a response-dominated freezing branch even when the intrinsic Majoron mass is larger than the present Hubble scale. The microscopic origin of the lag variable is identified with the phase-lagged off-diagonal coherence of the hidden pseudo-Dirac ensemble, while the response strength is controlled by a response-weighted hidden density rather than by an independent gravitating component. The resulting state is a metastable nonequilibrium frozen phase with \(w_φ\simeq -1\), rather than an exactly static cosmological constant.

</details>


### [8] [Flipped rotating axion: Baryogenesis and Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03085)
*Konstantinos Dimopoulos*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates that baryon asymmetry and dark matter can co-evolve from a rotating axion-like particle due to a change in the vacuum's orientation post-inflation, requiring specific gravitational coupling conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the mystery of dark matter and baryon asymmetry within a unified framework, using an axion-like particle influenced by inflation dynamics.

Method: The analysis involves examining a spectator axion-like particle that rotates post-inflation, requiring a periodic non-minimal gravitational coupling while maintaining shift symmetry, along with modeling the inflaton field's dynamics.

Result: The framework allows the rotating axion to generate baryon asymmetry via spontaneous baryogenesis, subsequently transitioning to act as dark matter through oscillations under specified coupling conditions.

Conclusion: The proposed coupling condition ($ξ	ext{∼}(f/m_P)^2$) is essential to prevent the fragmentation of the axion condensate, leading to a consistent framework for understanding dark matter and baryon asymmetry.

Abstract: It is shown that the co-genesis of baryon asymmetry and dark matter can be achieved through the rotation of a spectator axion-like particle, because of a flip in the vacuum manifold's orientation at the end of inflation. This can occur if the axion has a periodic non-minimal coupling to gravity (while preserving the discrete shift symmetry) in non-oscillating inflation models, where the inflaton field is characterised by a runaway potential. Our rotating axion can generate the baryon asymmetry of the Universe through spontaneous baryogenesis, while at a later epoch it can oscillate as dark matter. We show that in order to avoid fragmentation of the axion condensate during the rotation, we require the non-minimal coupling $ξ\sim(f/m_P)^2$, where $f$ is the axion decay constant.

</details>

### [10] [Transverse-spin dependent energy-energy correlators in proton-proton collisions within the dihadron fragmentation framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02441)
*Zhong-Bo Kang,Andreas Metz,Daniel Pitonyak,Congyue Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study presents a model for predicting energy-energy correlations in hadrons produced in transversely polarized proton-proton collisions, aligning with recent STAR measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore energy-energy correlations of hadrons in transversely polarized proton-proton collisions and assess the role of transversity parton distribution functions.

Method: Numerical predictions are derived from a model based on a global QCD analysis of dihadron fragmentation and transversity parton distributions.

Result: Results closely match recent STAR measurements, with notable consistency at high jet transverse momentum with lattice QCD computations of nucleon tensor charges.

Conclusion: The findings support a non-perturbative mechanism for near-side energy-energy correlators and emphasize their utility in probing transverse-spin effects within the nucleon.

Abstract: We calculate energy-energy correlations for two hadrons produced inside a jet in transversely polarized proton-proton collisions. We make numerical predictions based on a simple model that utilizes a previous global QCD analysis of dihadron fragmentation and transversity parton distribution functions. The results show remarkable agreement with a very recent STAR measurement. We also find the data at large jet transverse momentum have a slight preference for extractions of transversity that are consistent with lattice QCD computations of the nucleon tensor charges. Overall, this work provides further evidence for the underlying non-perturbative mechanism of near-side energy-energy correlators as well as highlights the potential for these observables to probe transverse-spin effects inside the nucleon.

</details>


### [11] [Polarized Nucleon as a Topological Dipole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03123)
*Kenji Fukushima,Tomoya Uji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper reveals that a polarized nucleon exhibits a dipole distribution of topological charge density, linked to the flavor-singlet axial charge, and illustrates this with a chiral soliton model while suggesting experimental avenues for verification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the topological charge density distribution in polarized nucleons and its implications in particle physics.

Method: Utilizing a two-flavor chiral soliton model that incorporates vector mesons and the $U(1)_A$ anomaly to demonstrate the emergence of the topological dipole from the soliton's rotation.

Result: The study confirms that the dipole strength is determined by the flavor-singlet axial charge and presents a dipole pattern resulting from a pseudoscalar profile.

Conclusion: Experimental strategies are proposed to investigate the findings, particularly through measuring exclusive production and asymmetries in heavy-ion collision scenarios.

Abstract: We show that a polarized nucleon generically carries a dipole distribution of topological charge density. This topological dipole follows robustly from the definition of the topological form factor and the pseudoscalar nature of the topological charge density. The strength of the topological dipole is fixed, in the chiral limit, by the flavor-singlet axial charge. We demonstrate the mechanism in a two-flavor chiral soliton model with vector mesons and the $U(1)_A$ anomaly, where the rotation of the soliton induces a singlet pseudoscalar profile and realizes the predicted dipole pattern. We also discuss possible experimental probes through exclusive $η$, $η^\prime$ production and directed-flow-like pseudoscalar-meson asymmetries correlated with magnetic fields or vorticity in relativistic heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [12] [A Unified Dark Matter Explanation for $\boldsymbol{B^+ \!\to K^+ν\barν}$ and the Super-Kamiokande Antineutrino Excess](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03206)
*Shu-Yu Ho,Jongkuk Kim,Pyungwon Ko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a complex scalar dark matter model to explain recent excesses observed in experiments, specifically focusing on neutrino events and branching fractions beyond Standard Model predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the intriguing excesses in experimental results that deviate from Standard Model expectations, particularly those from Super-Kamiokande and Belle II.

Method: The authors investigate a UV-complete complex scalar dark matter model incorporating a gauged U(1)_{	extsf{L}_μ- 	extsf{L}_τ} symmetry.

Result: The model effectively reproduces the observed dark matter relic density and aligns with both experimental excesses, providing a coherent explanation for the anomalies.

Conclusion: A light dark sector within this proposed framework can account for observed discrepancies in neutrino and branching fraction events, suggesting a potential new physics scenario.

Abstract: Recent results from Super-Kamiokande and Belle II have revealed intriguing excesses over Standard Model expectations. Super-Kamiokande observes a mild excess of $\barν_e^{}$-like events near $20\,\,\mathrm{MeV}$, while Belle II reports a branching fraction for $B^+ \!\to K^+ν\barν$ that exceeds the Standard Model prediction by approximately $2.7σ$. In this work, we study the simplest UV-complete complex scalar dark matter model with a gauged $\text{U}(1)_{\textsf{L}_μ- \textsf{L}_τ}^{}$ symmetry. We demonstrate that a light dark sector can simultaneously reproduce the observed dark matter relic density and accommodate both excesses within a unified framework.

</details>


### [13] [Probing a 146 GeV cLFV scalar using the LHC and low-energy experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03249)
*Christina Gao,Lingfeng Li,Z. J. Xiong*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The CMS Collaboration found a potential signal for lepton-flavor violation in Higgs decays at 146 GeV, and this paper assesses its compatibility with low-energy constraints, indicating future experimental tests will clarify its validity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the significance of a possible lepton-flavor-violating decay signal at 146 GeV seen by CMS, helping to confirm or refute evidence for charged lepton flavor violation.

Method: Employ a bottom-up effective field theory approach with a real scalar particle at 146 GeV, analyzing compatibility with various low-energy cLFV observables using Bayesian MCMC techniques.

Result: The analysis yields a preferred coupling value of approximately 10^{-4.09} for the cLFV process, constrained by current experimental limits like μ-e conversion.

Conclusion: Future experiments will either confirm or rule out this scalar particle interpretation of the CMS anomaly, ensuring a conclusive evaluation of charged lepton flavor violation.

Abstract: The CMS Collaboration reported a local excess at $146~\mathrm{GeV}$ in the search for the lepton-flavor-violating decay of the Higgs boson and additional Higgs bosons in the $eμ$ final state at $\sqrt{s}= 13~\mathrm{TeV}$. If confirmed, this would constitute a major piece of evidence of charged lepton flavor violation (cLFV). We investigate the compatibility of the claimed signal with the full suite of existing low-energy cLFV constraints in a bottom-up effective description: a single real scalar of mass $146~\mathrm{GeV}$ coupled to gluons and to all charged-lepton bilinears, with seven free parameters that simultaneously control the LHC production cross section, every di-lepton decay channel, and every low-energy cLFV observable. A Bayesian MCMC analysis against $μ-e$ conversion, muonium-antimuonium oscillation, three-lepton and radiative LFV decays, semileptonic $τ$ LFV decays, and LHC di-lepton searches yields a preferred mode with peaked value $Y_{eμ} \sim 10^{-4.09}$, already cut into by the current $μ-e$ conversion limits. The projected sensitivities of Mu2e, COMET, Mu3e, MACE, MEG~II, Belle~II, STCF, and the HL-LHC directly probe the region of coupling space selected by the CMS excess, so the complementarity between high-energy and low-energy cLFV probes will either corroborate or decisively exclude the scalar interpretation of the anomaly within the next decade.

</details>


### [14] [First look at the evaluation of two-loop Feynman integrals for radiative return processes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03343)
*Mattia Pozzoli,William J. Torres Bobadilla*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper details a method to calculate two-loop Feynman integrals crucial for radiative return processes at low-energy colliders, addressing analytical complexities and numerical stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance precision studies of radiative return processes in low-energy electron-positron colliders with accurate QED predictions incorporating electron mass.

Method: The authors calculate planar two-loop four-point Feynman integrals using differential equations that are polynomial in the dimensional regulator, facilitating numerical integration.

Result: They achieve stable numerical evaluations of the integrals across relevant physical regions, overcoming analytical complexities involving nested square roots and elliptic geometries.

Conclusion: The computed integrals serve as vital components for next-to-next-to-leading order predictions in radiative return processes.

Abstract: Precision studies of radiative return processes at low-energy electron--positron colliders require next-to-next-to-leading order QED predictions retaining full dependence on the electron mass. We present the calculation of planar two-loop four-point Feynman integrals relevant for initial-state radiation contributions to these processes. The calculation presents considerable analytical complexity, due to the presence of a nested square root and of integrals associated with elliptic geometries. We construct differential equations for the Feynman integrals which are polynomial in the dimensional regulator, and are suitable for numerical integration. We demonstrate stable numerical evaluations throughout the physical region relevant for low-energy experiments, despite the presence of large hierarchies of scales. Our results provide essential building blocks for NNLO predictions for radiative return processes.

</details>


### [15] [Analytic result of a three-loop integral family in the Higgs decay to four massive bottom quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03398)
*Jian Wang,Xing Wang,Yefan Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores the decay width of the Higgs particle decaying to four bottom quarks, utilizing three-loop Feynman diagrams and advanced mathematical techniques.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study aims to provide a deeper understanding of the decay processes involving the Higgs boson, particularly in complex multi-loop scenarios.

Method: Using the optical theorem and an algorithm from recent literature, the authors construct and solve the canonical differential equation for a family of integrals that account for various sectors and their interdependencies.

Result: The authors present analytical solutions for all master integrals in the family, achieving results up to the first two orders of the parameter ε.

Conclusion: This research advances techniques for tackling intricate integrals in particle physics, with implications for understanding Higgs decay processes.

Abstract: When calculating the decay width of $H\to b\bar{b}b\bar{b}$ induced by the effective Higgs-gluon-gluon interaction using the optical theorem, the three-loop Feynman diagrams are encountered. Among them, there is an interesting integral family, which involves not only the sectors evaluated to multiple polylogarithms but also those related to an elliptic curve and a K3 surface. We demonstrate how to construct and solve the canonical differential equation for the entire family, which includes mixing between different sectors, by using the recently proposed algorithm in~\cite{e-collaboration:2025frv, Bree:2025tug}. Analytical results for all master integrals in this family up to the first two orders of $\varepsilon$ are given.

</details>


### [16] [A Dispersive Look at Rare $B$-meson Semileptonic Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03531)
*Marco Ciuchini,Marco Fedele,Ayan Paul,Josua Scholze,Luca Silvestrini,Silvano Simula,Mauro Valli,Ludovico Vittorio*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes rare semileptonic $b 	o s$ transitions to test the Standard Model, focusing on hadronic uncertainties in form factors using a global analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide stringent tests of the Standard Model through rare semileptonic $b 	o s$ transitions, addressing hadronic uncertainties in form factors and matrix elements.

Method: A global analysis of $b 	o s 
ell^+
ell^-$ transitions using the Dispersive Matrix method, comparing lattice QCD and light-cone sum-rule inputs. Incorporates recent angular measurements and predictions for $b 	o s 
uar
u$ modes.

Result: The analysis indicates larger form factor uncertainties when using only lattice QCD, and supports long-distance hadronic effects rather than a short-distance shift in $C_9$ based on new fits.

Conclusion: The paper provides new SM predictions for $b 	o s 
uar
u$ modes and discusses implications for New Physics in light of recent experimental measurements, particularly from Belle~II.

Abstract: Rare semileptonic $b \to s$ flavour-changing neutral current transitions provide stringent tests of the Standard Model. Their interpretation is limited by hadronic uncertainties, notably the $B \to K^{(*)}$ and $B_s \to φ$ form factors (FFs) and the matrix elements of four-quark operators. We perform a global analysis of $b \to s \ell^+\ell^-$ transitions taking these uncertainties fully into account, determining the FFs through the Dispersive Matrix method and comparing a setup based solely on lattice QCD (LQCD) with one that also includes light-cone sum-rule (LCSR) inputs at low $q^2$. Compared to the case where both input are taken into account, using only LQCD substantially enlarges the FF uncertainties at large recoil. Combined with the latest LHCb and CMS angular measurements sensitive to strong phases, our global fit yields strengthened evidence in favour of long-distance hadronic effects rather than a short-distance shift in $C_9$. We further present new SM predictions for the theoretically clean $b \to s ν\barν$ modes, which depend only on local FFs, and a New Physics analysis of these transitions in the Weak Effective Theory, discussing their impact on the interpretation of the recent Belle~II measurement and on the available experimental upper bounds.

</details>


### [17] [Gravitational Wave Signatures of Cosmological Stasis: A Unified Spectral Template](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03537)
*Gabriela Barenboim,Anne-Katherine Burns*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a spectral template for inflationary gravitational waves during a stasis epoch in the early universe, linking observable quantities to the properties of the stasis phase.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a method for observing and falsifying predictions related to the stasis epoch in cosmology.

Method: A closed-form spectral template is derived based on the equation of state and stasis duration, yielding measurable observables like spectral tilt and amplitude step.

Result: The template connects observables in a way that allows for testing the theory against observational data, with a specific consistency curve relating spectral tilt and amplitude step.

Conclusion: The proposed detection method can identify deviations from the expected consistency curve, thus testing the validity of constant-$w$ cosmologies using data from gravitational wave experiments.

Abstract: Proposed in 2022 by Dienes et al., stasis is a dynamical fixed point in the early universe in which the equation of state, $w_s$, is fixed at a constant value. In this study we show that the inflationary gravitational wave imprint of any stasis epoch is captured by a closed-form spectral template controlled by two physical inputs, the equation of state $w_s$ and the stasis duration $ΔN_\mathrm{stasis}$, that applies uniformly across every microphysical realization. The template presented here yields two independently measurable observables, the spectral tilt of the spectra in the stasis band, $α(w_s)$ and the amplitude step $C^2(w_s)$ at the beginning and end of the stasis band. Eliminating $w_s$ gives a one-parameter consistency curve $C^2 = C^2(α)$ on which the data must lie if the underlying cosmology is any constant-$w$ era. This makes the spectrum falsifiable without knowing $w_s$ in advance: a measured $(α, C^2)$ pair either lands on the curve or rules out the constant-$w$ class. We show that BBO and DECIGO can resolve the perpendicular displacement from the consistency curve to $σ_\perp \simeq 1.5\times10^{-5}$ at a tensor-to-scalar ratio, $r = 0.01$, four orders of magnitude below the curve's range in $C^2$ meaning that any off-curve deviation is detectable across a broad range of allowed $r$ values.

</details>


### [18] [Spontaneous CP breaking in $S_3$ flavored Higgs sector with soft-breaking terms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03547)
*E. Barradas-Guevara,O. Félix-Beltrán,A. Pérez Martínez,E. Rodríguez-Jáuregui,J. Montaño-Peraza*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study explores the Higgs sector of a minimal $S_3$-invariant model, focusing on CP violation and Higgs dynamics involving three doublets.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To examine the implications of a minimal $S_3$-invariant extension of the Standard Model on CP violation and Higgs properties.

Method: Analysis of three SU(2) Higgs doublets with varied vacuum expectation values (vev's) and numerical investigations of the Higgs mass spectrum and trilinear self-couplings.

Result: Identification of a physical solution with spontaneous CP violation linked to the Higgs doublet $Φ_S$, revealing a rich parameter space for Higgs masses and couplings.

Conclusion: The model suggests one Higgs boson exhibits characteristics similar to the Standard Model Higgs boson, influenced by soft symmetry breaking and CP violation.

Abstract: We analyze the Higgs sector of the minimal $S_3$-invariant extension of the Standard Model including spontaneous and explicit CP violation arising from the spontaneous electroweak symmetry breaking. This extended Higgs sector includes three SU(2) Higgs doublets with and without complex vev's, which ones providing an interesting scenario to analyze the Higgs masses spectrum, trilinear Higgs self-couplings and CP violation. We present how the spontaneous electroweak symmetry breaking coming from three SU(2) Higgs doublets gives an interesting scenario when spontaneous CP violation arises from the Higgs doublet $Φ_S$, singlet under $S_3$. Furthermore, a numerical analysis of the Higgs masses and trilinear Higgs self-couplings is presented. Particularly, we find a physical solution for the scenario in which spontaneous CP break is provided by $Φ_S$. The analysis of soft breaking to $S_3$ symmetry including CP breaking terms opens up a rich parameter space, with Higgs masses and trilinear Higgs self-couplings favoring one of the Higgs bosons as a SM-like type Higgs boson.

</details>


### [19] [Probing Fermion-Portal Scalar Dark Matter through Charged Vector-Like Fermions at Future Muon Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03775)
*Songshaptak De,Tapoja Jha,Najimuddin Khan,Madhurima Pandey*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a fermion-portal scalar dark matter model with an emphasis on dark matter annihilation channels and explores the potential for discovering charged vector-like fermions at muon colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study addresses severe constraints on the Higgs-portal interaction due to new dark matter detection data, leading to the need for alternative routes to study dark matter interactions, particularly fermion-mediated channels.

Method: A detailed analysis of the model's parameter space was performed, incorporating constraints from various theoretical and experimental sources, and involved Yukawa-driven annihilation processes and co-annihilation between dark matter and new fermions.

Result: The study shows that an observed dark matter relic abundance can be achieved across various mass ranges while adhering to direct-detection limits and also assesses discovery potential for charged vector-like fermions at future muon colliders.

Conclusion: The analysis indicates that muon colliders can significantly enhance the discovery prospects of new fermions in this framework, providing promising avenues for exploring fermion-portal dark matter models.

Abstract: We revisit a minimal fermion-portal scalar dark matter model consisting of a real singlet scalar dark matter candidate and additional vector-like singlet and doublet charged fermions stabilized by a discrete $Z_2$ symmetry. In light of the latest dark matter direct-detection constraints, the conventional Higgs-portal interaction is severely restricted, motivating a detailed investigation of fermion-mediated dark matter annihilation channels. We perform a comprehensive analysis of the model parameter space by incorporating theoretical constraints from vacuum stability and perturbative unitarity, together with experimental bounds from relic density measurements, direct-detection experiments, Higgs invisible decay searches, lepton-flavor-violating processes, and anomalous magnetic moments. We show that the observed dark matter relic abundance can be successfully reproduced over a wide mass range through Yukawa-driven $t$- and $u$-annihilation and co-annihilation processes involving the new fermions, while remaining consistent with current direct-detection limits. Motivated by the viable parameter space, we investigate the discovery prospects of the lightest charged vector-like fermion at future muon colliders operating at center-of-mass energies of 3 TeV and 10 TeV. Focusing on the process $μ^+μ^- \to E_1^+E_1^- \to e^+e^- + \cancel{E}_T$, we perform a detector-level analysis including realistic Standard Model backgrounds. We demonstrate that the clean experimental environment of a muon collider provides excellent sensitivity to charged fermion masses extending into the multi-TeV regime, significantly improving the exploration prospects of this class of fermion-portal dark matter scenarios.

</details>


### [20] [Lagrangian-based model of the $e^+e^- \to V \bar V$ process applied to the $e^+e^- \to D^{\ast+} D^{\ast-}$ data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04210)

Result: Validation of the formula through fit to $e^+ e^- 	o D^{ullet}D^{	ext{*}}$ data, confirming its effectiveness.

Conclusion: The derived formula accurately describes the annihilation process and is supported by experimental evidence.

Abstract: Using the quantum field theory, we derive a Breit-Wigner-type formula for the $e^+ e^-$ annihilation into a vector meson and its antiparticle, and relate the formula parameters to observable quantities. The formula's soundness is checked by fitting the $e^+ e^- \to D^{\ast+}D^{\ast-}$ data published by the BESIII Collaboration in 2022.

### [21] [Dark matter scattering with pre-supernova neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03943)
*Sangeetha N. Tallur,Nirmal Raj*

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how pre-supernova neutrinos can shed light on the interactions between dark matter and neutrinos.

Method: Analyzing the flux of electron anti-neutrinos and their interactions with dark matter, and predicting the event rate in large-volume detectors.

Result: A predicted deficit of inverse beta decay events, leading to new upper limits on dark matter-neutrino scattering cross sections, alongside potential cross-checks with existing limits.

Conclusion: Pre-supernova neutrinos offer a valuable experimental avenue to investigate dark matter interactions and can validate or challenge current models.

Abstract: Pre-supernova neutrinos, emitted during the last day prior to core collapse of a massive star, could provide a unique probe at MeV energies of scattering interactions between dark matter and neutrinos. Due to attenuation of the flux of electron anti-neutrinos from their scattering on dark matter on the way to Earth, we expect a deficit of inverse beta decay events at large-volume, low-threshold detectors such as KamLAND, JUNO, and Super-K. which we use to derive upper limits on the dark matter-neutrino reduced scattering cross section. Though seemingly weaker than limits from post-bounce supernova neutrino events, these sensitivities provide an important cross-check, could help determine the energy dependence of the cross section, and may even be a distinct probe of certain models. Further, pre-supernova neutrinos may test hints reported of dark matter-neutrino scattering in the early universe suppressing small-scale power as seen in Lyman-alpha data.

</details>


### [22] [Phenomenological extraction of fragmentation functions in a $p\bar{p}$ environment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04059)
*M. A. Pérez de León,S. A. Ochoa-Oregón,D. F. Rentería-Estrada,R. J. Hernández-Pinto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the extraction of fragmentation functions (FFs) of hadrons using kinematical cuts in proton-antiproton collisions, comparing it to methodologies used in proton-proton collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The accurate extraction of FFs is crucial for understanding high-energy hadron collisions and relies on precise differential cross section measurements from experiments and theoretical predictions.

Method: The authors analyze the use of kinematical cuts in pion and kaon reactions within proton-antiproton collisions, focusing on photon-hadron production and employing NLO QCD and LO QED corrections.

Result: The study highlights the potential for isolating individual FF contributions and discusses the feasibility of applying proton-proton collider techniques to proton-antiproton settings.

Conclusion: This work contributes to refining the methodologies for FF extraction, potentially enhancing the precision of theoretical predictions in high-energy physics.

Abstract: The precise determination of fragmentation functions (FFs) of hadrons relies on the accurate description of the differential cross sections obtained from both experimental high-energy hadron colliders and theoretical predictions at higher orders in quantum chromodynamics. Various phenomenological strategies have been employed to extract FFs. In this work, we analyze the use of kinematical cuts for reactions including pions and kaons in proton-antiproton collisions to isolate individual FF contributions. This study examines the feasibility of using a similar approach as in proton-proton colliders to analyze FF flavour separation~\cite{Ochoa-Oregon:2023ktx}. In particular, we study photon-hadron production at colliders, including NLO QCD and LO QED corrections to reconstruct the partonic momentum fractions.

</details>


### [23] [Exclusive production of P-wave charmonia through two virtual photons in electron-positron annihilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04126)
*Avinash Okram,Shashank Bhatnagar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a relativistic study of exclusive double-charmonium production in $e^-e^+$ collisions, predicting cross sections and their dependencies using the Bethe-Salpeter equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore exclusive double-charmonium processes as a means to investigate relativistic quarkonium dynamics.

Method: The study employs the $4\times4$ Bethe-Salpeter equation to analyze processes $e^-e^+ \to γ^*γ^* \to χ_{c1}+η_c$ and $e^-e^+ \to γ^*γ^* \to h_c+h_c$, using fixed parameters from previous mass spectroscopy work.

Result: Predicted total cross sections are approximately $10^{-3}$ fb and show a hierarchy based on meson wave function structure, with smooth power-law falloff for both processes.

Conclusion: The findings offer benchmark predictions for future studies at high-luminosity $e^-e^+$ colliders, aligning qualitatively with NRQCD expectations.

Abstract: We present a relativistic study of the exclusive double-charmonium processes $e^-e^+ \to γ^*γ^* \to χ_{c1}+η_c$ and $e^-e^+ \to γ^*γ^* \to h_c+h_c$ at $\sqrt{s}=10.6$ GeV in the framework of the $4\times4$ Bethe-Salpeter equation. Since these channels are forbidden in single-photon annihilation, they proceed purely through two-photon quark-rearrangement mechanisms and thus provide a clean probe of relativistic quarkonium dynamics. Using parameters fixed from mass spectroscopy in our earlier work, we obtain leading-order predictions for the total cross sections and their energy dependence. The predicted cross sections are of order $10^{-3}$ fb and exhibit a hierarchy that originates from the radial structure of the meson wave functions, which determines the overlap integrals contributing to the production amplitude. While the absolute magnitudes of the cross sections differ from NRQCD estimates, the qualitative ordering of the channels is consistent with NRQCD expectations. We find a smooth power-law falloff of the cross section for both $χ_{c1}+η_c$ and $h_c+h_c$. These results provide benchmark predictions for future searches at high-luminosity $e^-e^+$ colliders.

</details>


### [24] [Anomalous triple gauge couplings in the light of dimension-8 operators in $W^+W^-$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04251)
*Daniel Gillies,Andrea Banfi,Adam Martin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes dimension-8 effects on $W^+W^-$ production at the LHC from $qar q$ and $γγ$ initial states, highlighting the significance of bosonic operators and their contribution to gauge couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the impact of dimension-8 operators on $W^+W^-$ production and set constraints on these higher dimensional operators using experimental data.

Method: The analysis involves matching dimension-6 and dimension-8 operators to anomalous triple gauge couplings, employing MCFM-RE for the $qar q$ channel and MadGraph for the $γγ$ channel, including resummation techniques for large logarithmic contributions.

Result: The $γγ$ channel exhibits a dominant bosonic dimension-8 contribution, highlighting its importance for evaluating Effective Field Theory validity, while neglecting fermionic operators.

Conclusion: The study offers constraints on higher dimensional operators by incorporating both theoretical and experimental uncertainties, utilizing current and future LHC data.

Abstract: We compare the size of dimension-8 effects on $W^+W^-$ production at the LHC arising from the $q\bar q$ and $γγ$ initial states. In particular, we consider bosonic operators, which contribute to the anomalous triple and quadruple gauge couplings. The relevant dimension-6 and dimension-8 operators are matched to the anomalous triple gauge couplings that contribute to the $q\bar q$ channel, allowing for the resummation of large logarithmic contributions arising in the presence of a jet-veto through the program MCFM-RE. For the $γγ$ channel, which receives contributions from both triple and quadruple gauge couplings, such resummation can be performed through the program MadGraph, by simply setting the factorisation scale to the jet-veto scale. We find that, when neglecting fermionic dimension-8 operators, the $γγ$ channel has a dominant bosonic dimension-8 contribution and this channel can be used to understand the range of validity of the Effective Field Theory. With this in mind, and carefully considering theoretical and experimental uncertainties, we provide constraints on higher dimensional operators using current and future data.

</details>


### [25] [Probing axion in the DFSZ model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04128)
*H. N. Long,N. H. T. Nha*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduction of new right-handed neutrinos in the DFSZ model alters the $PQ$ transformation, isolating it to the scalar singlet's charge and influencing the model's scalar fields and couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how new right-handed neutrinos impact the Peccei–Quinn transformation and the associated scalar fields in the DFSZ model.

Method: The study presents a model with specific new scalar particles and analyzes their couplings, emphasizing the separation of axion and Goldstone boson mixing via unitary matrices.

Result: The model exhibits consistency with experimental constraints when analyzing the mass range of new scalar fields and their corresponding couplings.

Conclusion: The findings provide a deeper understanding of the couplings in the DFSZ model, particularly in relation to new scalars and the implications for new physics.

Abstract: We show that with the introduction of new right-handed neutrinos $N_R$, the Peccei--Quinn ($PQ$) transformation in the DFSZ (Dine--Fischler--Srednicki--Zhitnitsky) model depends only on the $PQ$ charge of the scalar singlet $φ$, namely $PQ_φ$, and is independent of the sine and cosine of $\arctan(v_u/v_d)$. The model consists of four new scalars: a charged Higgs boson $H^\pm$, a CP-odd scalar $A$, and two CP-even scalars with masses at the EW scale (of a few hundred GeV), and a superheavy inflaton $Φ$. The scalar fields have been presented in the form of unitary matrices, in which there is no mixing between the axion and the Goldstone boson $G_Z$. As a result, there are two kinds of couplings between the axion and fermions, namely Yukawa-like interactions and anomaly-induced ones associated with derivative axion couplings. {The model belongs to the DFSZ I kind since the axion - photon coupling with $\fr E N = \fr 8 3$.} The trilinear Higgs self-coupling is investigated. We have shown that, in the parameter region where new scalar fields such as the singly charged Higgs boson $H^\pm$ and the CP-odd scalar $A$ have masses around 150 GeV and the vacuum expectation value of one Higgs doublet ($v_u$) is of the order of a few GeV, the coupling modifier $κ_λ$-a probe of new physics-is consistent with the current experimental constraints.

</details>


### [26] [Quantum interference effects enhanced in $π^+p$ femtoscopic correlation functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04351)
*Zhi-Wei Liu,Jun-Xu Lu,Duo-Lun Ge,Li-Sheng Geng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes $π^+p$ femtoscopic correlation functions from high-multiplicity $pp$ collisions using the Koonin-Pratt formula, revealing new insights into quantum interference and addressing a peak-shift puzzle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the femtoscopic correlation functions of $π^+p$ in high-energy proton-proton collisions, and address the peak-shift puzzle observed in previous studies.

Method: The Koonin-Pratt formula with Gaussian sources and data-driven $πN$ partial-wave amplitudes is utilized to analyze correlations, accounting for both scattering and decay contributions.

Result: The analysis reveals a scattering contribution peak at $k	ext{≈}140$ MeV/c and a decay contribution peak at $k	ext{≈}220$ MeV/c, with the correlation resulting from a weighted sum of both contributions with $m_T$-dependent weights; quantum interference between the waves is highlighted.

Conclusion: New insights into quantum interference in femtoscopic studies are provided, successfully resolving the peak-shift puzzle in $π^+p$ correlations.

Abstract: We present a comprehensive analysis of the $π^+p$ femtoscopic correlation functions measured by the ALICE Collaboration in high-multiplicity $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV. Using the Koonin-Pratt formula with a Gaussian source and data-driven $πN$ partial-wave amplitudes, we account for the contributions from $π^+p$ scattering and $Δ(1232)^{++}$-decay, thereby successfully reproducing the measured data and their transverse-mass ($m_T$) dependence. The scattering contribution yields a peak near the relative momentum $k\approx140$ MeV/$c$, whereas the decay contribution peaks around $k\approx220$ MeV/$c$. The observed correlation peak results from a weighted sum of the two contributions, with $m_T$-dependent relative weights. We find that the 140 MeV/$c$ peak originates from quantum interference between the incident and scattered waves-a mechanism previously unnoticed in femtoscopic studies. This finding resolves the peak-shift puzzle in $π^+p$ correlations and provides a novel perspective for quantum interference effects in femtoscopy.

</details>


### [27] [Left-right symmetry breaking in $E_6^L\times E_6^R$ occurs only in spacetime -- with possible implications for strong $CP$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04132)
*Tejinder P. Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a unification model involving two-sided octonionic symmetry and argues that a second color gauge group does not require suppression, as left-right symmetry operates in spacetime rather than internally. The paper proves an anomaly no-go theorem related to visible fermions and discusses implications for charge and parity in the dark sector and in observable physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore a unification scheme incorporating two-sided octonionic symmetry while clarifying misconceptions regarding the necessity of suppressing additional internal color symmetries.

Method: The authors use symmetry arguments related to left-right spacetime operations, proving an anomaly no-go theorem and analyzing implications for gauge groups and physical observables.

Result: The findings indicate that the additional gauged color symmetry can be identified with the standard color charge without needing external suppression, and elucidate charge dynamics in both visible and dark sectors.

Conclusion: The study concludes with implications for QCD vacuum angles, CKM phase relations, and highlights the modular relationship between derived quantities and open questions in particle physics.

Abstract: Two-sided octonionic $E_6^L\times E_6^R$ unification carries a nominal second colour $SU(3)_{c'}$ which, gauged, would colour the charged lepton and must be suppressed by hand. We argue no such device is needed: the left-right symmetry acts in spacetime, not internally -- in the gravi-weak reading $SU(2)_R$ is the gravitational frame group and the exchange is ordinary parity on the Dirac field; a spacetime operation cannot double the internal colour, so $SU(3)_{c'}=SU(3)_c$, one vector-like colour with a colour-singlet electron. The right sector contributes one colour-blind datum, $\sqrt m$ (values: the exceptional-Jordan trace split, an input; $N_R$ fixes only the colour representation). An anomaly no-go proven here shows every anomaly-free $U(1)$ on visible fermions lies in span$\{Q,B-L\}$, which the $\sqrt m$ pattern does not, in any sign or chirality assignment. The gauged dark-electromagnetic $U(1)_{dem}$ -- the mirror electroweak chain's unbroken remnant -- therefore carries the parity-mirror of electric charge on the dark sector: the visible fermions are dem-neutral (kinetic mixing the sole portal; no visible fifth force), and $\sqrt m$ enters visible physics only as the spectral label of the Jordan mass operator. The same recognition recovers hypercharge as the consistency relation $Y=Q-T^3_L$, $Q=N/3$, with no right-sector generator. As an application, the spontaneous parity forbids the QCD vacuum angle ($θ_{QCD}=0$, conditional on the gravi-weak identification), while the flavour rotors have real determinant, so $\arg\det M=0$ at tree level (exact for the Cabibbo rung, texture-contingent in full), coexisting with a nonzero CKM phase. Loop stability -- where minimal gauged-$SU(2)_R$ parity solutions fail -- is plausibly evaded (no gauged $W_R$) but open, pending the Higgs-bridge matrix elements. We tag throughout what is derived, what is input, and what is open.

</details>


### [28] [Charting doubly strange hidden-charm pentaquarks: An electromagnetic mapping of spin-$\frac{1}{2}$ and $\frac{3}{2}$ states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04355)
*Ulaş Özdem*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study calculates magnetic dipole moments and other multipole moments for doubly strange hidden-charm pentaquark states using QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSR), highlighting significant model dependence and implications for future research.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically investigate the electromagnetic multipole structure of doubly strange pentaquark states, contributing to the understanding of their internal dynamics and properties.

Method: Utilized QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSR) with different interpolating currents in diquark-diquark-antiquark configurations to evaluate magnetic dipole, electric quadrupole, and magnetic octupole moments.

Result: Findings showed a considerable range of magnetic dipole moments across different currents, with charm quarks dominating most configurations and significant dependence on the choice of interpolating operator.

Conclusion: These predictions provide critical benchmarks for future experimental validation and lattice QCD calculations, aiding in distinguishing among various structural models of pentaquarks.

Abstract: We calculate the magnetic dipole moments of doubly strange hidden-charm pentaquark states with spin-parity $J^P = 1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ using QCD light-cone sum rules (LCSR), presenting the first systematic LCSR investigation of the electromagnetic multipole structure in the $S = -2$ sector. To assess the model dependence, we employ independent interpolating currents in diquark-diquark-antiquark form, which probe different assumptions about the internal color-spin correlations. For the spin-$3/2$ states, we also compute the electric quadrupole and magnetic octupole moments. The magnetic dipole moments exhibit a considerable spread across currents, ranging from $-2.15\,μ_N$ to $5.74\,μ_N$ for spin-$1/2$ pentaquarks and from $-4.25\,μ_N$ to $-0.43\,μ_N$ for spin-$3/2$ states, reflecting the sensitivity of magnetic moments to the internal wave function. A quark-level decomposition reveals that the charm quark dominates in most configurations, while strange quarks play a decisive role only in currents favoring axial-vector diquark structures. The electric quadrupole moments lie between $-2.01\times10^{-2}$ fm$^2$ and $5.55\times10^{-2}$ fm$^2$, and the magnetic octupole moments are typically an order of magnitude smaller. The current dependence of the magnetic dipole moments provides a quantitative measure of the theoretical uncertainty arising from the choice of interpolating operator. The pronounced isospin sensitivity of $J^3_μ(x)$ across all three multipole moments arises from its axial-vector diquark structure, which isolates the light quark from spin averaging and allows the charge asymmetry $e_u/e_d = -2$ to propagate directly into the electromagnetic moments; the ratio $μ_u/μ_d = -2.00$ confirms this mechanism exactly. Our predictions offer benchmarks for future experiments and lattice QCD calculations, and may help discriminate among competing structural models.

</details>


### [29] [Naturally Light Composite Higgs as a Protected Collective Eigenmode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04136)
*Gauhar Abbas*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a new mechanism for achieving a light composite Higgs using the eigenvalue of a multi-operator scalar kernel, introducing diagnostics that help differentiate protected collective Higgs from accidental modes and discusses implications for technicolor and fermion mass dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing approaches to light composite Higgs require fine-tuning or rely on specific symmetries, prompting the need for alternative mechanisms that can protect Higgs-like states.

Method: Introduces a new category of protected collective Higgs characterized by sector diagnostics and sensitivity measures, providing conditions under which this class is established and discussing theoretical implications.

Result: Identifies three key characteristics of a protected collective Higgs: high sector gap ratio, participation in the strong sector, and moderate sensitivity to coupling constants, proving this mechanism can exist without prior fine-tuning.

Conclusion: The proposed framework allows exploration within particle physics models, specifically through experimental techniques like lattice spectroscopy, making the scenario testable against empirical data.

Abstract: Standard routes to a light composite Higgs either rely on tuning a single channel near criticality or protect a pseudo-Nambu--Goldstone coordinate of a coset. We introduce a third mechanism class in which the protected object is an \emph{eigenvalue} of the renormalized multi-operator scalar kernel of the strong sector. Two basis-invariant diagnostics, a sector participation number $\Psec$ and a sector gap ratio $\Rcoll$, identify collective lightness, but they cannot distinguish an accidental small determinant from a protected zero mode; the missing discriminator is the microscopic sensitivity $Δ_g=\left|\partial\ln|m_H^2|/\partial\ln g\right|$. A protected collective Higgs is defined by $\Rcoll\gg1$, $\Psec>1$, and $Δ_g=\mathcal{O}(1)$. We prove that this class is nonempty. A rank-one TC--DTC locking invariant forbids tree-level aligned curvature, while universal vectorlike DQCD bridge fermions, massless in the microscopic Lagrangian but acquiring a common DQCD constituent mass, obey $\partial_h^2\sum_A\Tr\mathcal{M}_A^2\big|_0=0$. The aligned scalar is therefore lifted only at joint two-spurion order, $m^2_{\mathrm{br}}=-(d_X/2π^2)g_T^2g_D^2(f_B^4/f_H^2)L_X$, giving $Δ_{g_T}=Δ_{g_D}=2$ at leading logarithmic order. The same DTC topology admits a collective top completion and a vector-decoupling route to reducing the positive technicolor contribution to $S$. The mechanism is falsifiable by sector-restricted lattice spectroscopy and coupling-response scans.

</details>


### [30] [Probing Neutrinophilic Long-Range Forces at DUNE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04441)
*Sudip Jana,Pragyanprasu Swain*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates a dark neutrino portal linking neutrino mass generation to a light dark sector mediated by a new $U(1)_D$ gauge symmetry, exploring implications for neutrino oscillations and potential insights from the DUNE experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore physics beyond the Standard Model and utilize neutrinos as probes of new interactions and hidden sectors, particularly through dark neutrino scenarios.

Method: The authors analyze a model in which neutrinos mix with a dark sector charged under a $U(1)_D$ gauge symmetry, leading to ultra-long-range interactions that modify neutrino oscillations.

Result: The study finds that the DUNE experiment can probe previously unexplored regions of parameter space, revealing sizable neutrino self-interactions that may address current discrepancies in cosmological observations, notably the Hubble tension.

Conclusion: The proposed dark neutrino portal model is consistent with current constraints and has the potential to provide significant insights into neutrino physics and cosmology.

Abstract: Neutrino oscillations provide compelling evidence for physics beyond the Standard Model, while the weakly interacting nature of neutrinos makes them powerful probes of new interactions and hidden sectors. In this work, we investigate a \textit{dark neutrino portal} scenario in which neutrino mass generation is linked to a light dark sector charged under a new $U(1)_D$ gauge symmetry. While Standard Model fields remain neutral under $U(1)_D$, the dark neutrino sector is charged and communicates with the Standard Model exclusively through active--dark neutrino mixing. The associated neutrinophilic mediator induces ultra-long-range interactions, whereby electrons and neutrons in the Earth, Moon, Sun, Milky Way, and the cosmological matter distribution generate sizable matter potentials that modify neutrino oscillations. We explore the sensitivity of the upcoming Deep Underground Neutrino Experiment (DUNE), whose long baseline and pronounced matter effects make it uniquely suited to probe such interactions. We show that DUNE can access previously unexplored regions of parameter space and demonstrate that the same underlying coupling can simultaneously give rise to sizable neutrino self-interactions, including regions relevant for alleviating the Hubble tension, while remaining consistent with current neutrino oscillation constraints.

</details>


### [31] [A MeV-Scale Dark QCD Solution to the Axion Domain Wall Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04137)
*Jun Guo,Zhaofeng Kang,Jiang Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes a solution to the axion domain wall problem in the context of the PQ solution to the strong CP problem by introducing mixed anomalies, which alters effective angles and proposes observational signatures involving gravitational waves and di-photon signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the axion domain wall problem that arises when considering the PQ solution to the strong CP problem.

Method: Introducing mixed anomalies to a hidden $SU(N_c)$ color and deriving a new cosine potential from hidden instantons, which alters the potential landscape of axions and leads to observable phenomena.

Result: The new potential modifies the vacuum structure and the effective $θ$ angle, posing challenges to the solution of the strong CP problem while maintaining dark QCD scales; specific signatures such as gravitational waves and di-photon signals are identified.

Conclusion: The proposed scenario supports a cold dark QCD sector compatible with existing constraints on dark glueballs, highlighting potential observational tests.

Abstract: PQ solution to the strong CP problem probably encounters the axion domain wall problem. In this article, we propose a simple and testable solution, assuming that the $U(1)_{\rm PQ}$ possesses mixed anomaly to a hidden $SU(N_c)$ color. Then, the axion field receives a new cosine potential from the hidden instantons, which breaks the $Z_{N_{\rm DW}}$ subgroup explicitly. The new potential lifts the vacua degeneracy, but also drives the effective $θ$ angle away from the origin, re-incuring the strong CP problem. However, we find that the dark QCD scale within the 0.1 to 3 MeV window survives, maintaining a delicate balance. Two observational signatures are explored: gravitational waves from domain wall collapse, already probed by current PTAs, and di-photon signals from axion-dark-glueball mixing, which require next-generation MeV telescopes. The scenario favors a cold dark QCD sector consistent with dark glueball relic constraints.

</details>


### [32] [Implications of exclusive photon leptoproduction measurements for the proton charge-radius puzzle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04481)
*The MMGPDs Collaboration,Muhammad Goharipour,Anoushiravan Moradi,K. Azizi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study revisits proton electromagnetic form factors using extensive EP data, revealing inconsistencies in measurements and proposing methods to derive stable proton radii while addressing the charge-radius puzzle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address inconsistencies in proton electromagnetic form factors derived from exclusive photon leptoproduction (EP) measurements and to refine the determination of proton charge and magnetic radii.

Method: Utilized a phenomenological framework to analyze high-precision EP data from CLAS and Hall A, testing different fitting scenarios to extract proton electromagnetic form factors and their corresponding radii.

Result: Significant tension was found between CLAS 2018 data and other measurements; excluding or restricting this data led to consistent fitting results. The extracted proton charge radius was smaller than the Particle Data Group average but aligned with certain other measurements, while the magnetic radius matched the current world average.

Conclusion: The study highlights that BH-dominated EP measurements can independently inform proton structure and reinforce the validity of the smaller charge radius in resolving the proton charge-radius puzzle.

Abstract: In the present study, we extend our previous analysis of the proton electromagnetic form factors (FFs) extracted from exclusive photon leptoproduction (EP) measurements in kinematic regions where the Bethe-Heitler (BH) process dominates the cross section by including all currently available high-precision EP data from the CLAS and Hall~A Collaborations. Using the same phenomenological framework, we investigate the consistency among the different data sets, determine the proton electromagnetic FFs within several fitting scenarios, and extract the corresponding charge and magnetic radii. A significant tension is observed between the CLAS 2018 measurements and the remaining EP data. We show that excluding this data set, or restricting its kinematic coverage by imposing suitable low-$|t|$ cuts, leads to stable fits with good quality and consistent FFs. For all analyses, the extracted proton charge radius is smaller than the Particle Data Group average and most determinations based on elastic electron-proton scattering. However, the results are consistent, within uncertainties, with the PRad measurement and muonic hydrogen spectroscopy. In contrast, the magnetic radius is found to be compatible with the current world average. These results demonstrate that BH-dominated EP measurements provide an independent and complementary approach to determine the electromagnetic structure of the proton and offer additional support for the small-radius solution of the proton charge-radius puzzle.

</details>


### [33] [Advancing axion detection: Photon regeneration in high-sensitivity Penning trap experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04155)

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study proposes a novel experimental approach to detect axions using photon regeneration and Penning trap technologies, achieving high sensitivity in measuring axion-photon couplings.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need for effective detection methods for axions, which may explain the strong CP problem and act as dark matter, drives this research.

Method: The method combines photon regeneration techniques with Penning trap technologies to enhance sensitivity in axion-photon coupling measurements, particularly using resonantly enhanced microwave cavities.

Result: The proposed method achieves a coupling constant limit of $g_{aγγ} 	extless 7.10 \times 10^{-8}\mathrm{GeV^{-1}}$ in one day, targeting axion energies below 1 MHz.

Conclusion: This approach represents a significant improvement in laboratory searches for axions, potentially advancing our understanding of dark matter while avoiding astrophysical uncertainties.

Abstract: The axion, a hypothetical particle proposed to solve the strong CP problem and considered a viable candidate for dark matter, has prompted extensive experimental efforts for its detection. This study presents a novel approach combining photon regeneration techniques ("light-shining-through-a-wall") with high-sensitivity Penning trap technologies to enhance the search for axions. Penning traps offer significant advantages, including precise electromagnetic field measurement, strong magnetic fields, and single-particle detection capabilities. By integrating these traps with resonantly enhanced photon regeneration using microwave cavities, our proposed method significantly increases sensitivity to axion-photon couplings. Preliminary calculations demonstrate an unprecedented achievable sensitivity, reaching an axion-photon coupling constant limit of $g_{aγγ} \le 7.10\times 10^{-8}\mathrm{GeV ^{-1}}$ in just one day, specifically targeting axion energies below 1 MHz. This experimental setup presents a robust and controlled platform, circumventing astrophysical uncertainties, and represents a substantial advancement in laboratory searches for axions and our understanding of dark matter.

</details>
